fills a surface of Euler characteristic
\geq-C_{\kappa}
. Hence, the sum in
Eq.

10.21
can be reduced
to geodesics filling a surface of Euler characteristic
\geq-C_{\kappa}
.

For every filling type
S
such that
2\leq|\chi_{S}|\leq C_{\kappa}
, we
use Wu–Xue’s counting result,
2.3
, together with

5.20
for
K=|\chi_{S}|
to obtain that

(10.22)

\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[\sum_{\gamma:S_{\gamma}=S}\frac{\ell_{X}(\gamma)\,H_{L,m}(\ell_{X}(\gamma))}{\exp\left(\frac{\ell_{X}(\gamma)}{2}\right)}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\kappa}(X)}\Bigg]=\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon}\left(\frac{e^{(1+\epsilon)L/2}}{g^{2}}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon}\left(g^{1+3\epsilon}\right).

The number of such filling types is
\mathcal{O}\left(C_{\kappa}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\kappa}\left(1\right)
. As a
consequence, equations (
10.21
) and
(
10.22
) together imply that
\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\big[\hat{H}_{L,m}(r_{1})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\kappa}}\big]
is smaller than

(10.23)

\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[\sum_{\gamma:|\chi_{S_{\gamma}}|\leq 1}\frac{\ell_{X}(\gamma)\,H_{L,m}(\ell_{X}(\gamma))}{\exp\left(\frac{\ell_{X}(\gamma)}{2}\right)}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\kappa}(X)}\Bigg]+\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon}\left(g^{1+3\epsilon}\right).

We shall now split this sum by local topological type. In order to do so in a
controlled way, we apply
10.4
. This gives us a set

\mathrm{Loc}_{g}^{\kappa}
of local topologies such that, as soon as

X\in\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\kappa}
, all closed geodesics shorter than
6\log g
filling a
pair of pants or once-holed torus are locally equivalent to a type in

\mathrm{Loc}_{g}^{\kappa}
, and

(10.24)

\#\mathrm{Loc}_{g}^{\kappa}=\mathcal{O}_{\kappa}\left((\log g)^{c_{\kappa}}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\kappa,\epsilon}\left(g^{\epsilon}\right).

The bound (
10.23
) can then be rewritten as

(10.25)

\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\big[\hat{H}_{L,m}(r_{1})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\kappa}}\big]\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\in\mathrm{Loc}_{g}^{\kappa}\\
\text{ or }T=\text{ ``simple''}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Av}_{g,m,\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{O}_{\kappa,\epsilon}\left(g^{1+3\epsilon}\right)

where for any
T\in\mathrm{Loc}_{g}^{\kappa}\cup\{\text{simple}\}
,

(10.26)

\mathrm{Av}_{g,m,\kappa}(T):=\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in T^{g}\\
\ell_{X}(\gamma)\leq 6\log(g)\end{subarray}}\frac{\ell_{X}(\gamma)\,H_{L,m}(\ell_{X}(\gamma))}{\exp\left(\frac{\ell_{X}(\gamma)}{2}\right)}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\kappa}(X)}\Bigg].

Let
T\in\mathrm{Loc}_{g}^{\kappa}\cup\{\mathrm{simple}\}
.

•

If
T
fills a once-holed torus, then by
8.9
,
T

is a double-filling loop. We then bound naively

(10.27)

|\mathrm{Av}_{g,m,\kappa}(T)|\leq\left\langle|H_{L,m}(\ell)|\,\ell\,e^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\right\rangle_{g}^{{T}}

and use our asymptotic expansion,
5.14
, at the order

2
with
\epsilon^{\prime}=\epsilon/2
, to write

\left\langle|H_{L,m}(\ell)|\,\ell\,e^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\right\rangle_{g}^{{T}}
as

\int_{0}^{+\infty}|H_{L,m}(\ell)|e^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\left(\ell f_{0}^{T}(\ell)+\frac{\ell f_{1}^{T}(\ell)}{g}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\ell+\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon}\left(\frac{\|\ell H_{L,m}(\ell)e^{\frac{(1+\epsilon)\ell}{2}}\|_{\infty}}{g^{2}}\right).

The remainder is
\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon,m}\left(g^{1+4\epsilon}\right)
by definition of

H_{L,m}
and since
L=6\log(g)
. By
8.6
, there
exists a constant
c_{1}>0
independent of
T
such that
f_{0}^{T}
and
f_{1}^{T}

belong in
\mathcal{R}_{w}
, and their exponents and constants are smaller than

c_{1}
. It then follows directly by the definition of
\mathcal{R}_{w}
that

(10.28)

\mathrm{Av}_{g,m,\kappa}(T)=\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon,m}\left(\|(\ell+1)^{c_{1}+1}H_{L,m}\|_{\infty}+g^{1+4\epsilon}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon,m}\left(g^{1+4\epsilon}\right).

•

If
T
is simple or fills a pair of pants, we apply

10.12
. We obtain that there exists a constant
c_{2}>0

such that

(10.29)

\mathrm{Av}_{g,m,\kappa}(T)=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{D}^{m}h_{L}(\ell)\,e^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\,\ell A_{T,g}^{\kappa}(\ell)\,\mathrm{d}\ell+\mathcal{O}_{\kappa,m}\left(g^{1+c_{2}\kappa}\right),

where
\ell\mapsto\ell A_{T,g}^{\kappa}(\ell)
is a Friedman–Ramanujan
function of degree and exponent
\leq c_{2}
and norm and constant

\mathcal{O}_{\kappa}\left(g^{c_{2}\kappa}\right)
. We now specify the value of the parameter
m

to be
m:=\lceil c_{2}\rceil+1
, so that we can use the cancellation
properties of Friedman–Ramanujan functions. More precisely, by

3.16
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{D}^{m}h_{L}(\ell)\,e^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\ell A_{T,g}^{\kappa}(\ell)\,\mathrm{d}\ell=\mathcal{O}_{\kappa}\left(g^{c_{2}\kappa}(L+1)^{c_{2}+1}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon,\kappa}\left(g^{2\epsilon}\right)

as soon as
\kappa<\epsilon/c_{2}
.
Together with (
10.29
), this implies that for small enough
\kappa
,

(10.30)

\mathrm{Av}_{g,m,\kappa}(T)=\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon,\kappa}\left(g^{1+\epsilon}\right).

Then, equations (
10.24
), (
10.28
)
and (
10.30
) together imply that

\sum_{T\in\mathrm{Loc}_{g}^{\kappa}\cup\{\mathrm{simple}\}}\mathrm{Av}_{g,m,\kappa}(T)=\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon,\kappa}\left(g^{\epsilon+1+4\epsilon}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon}\left(g^{1+5\epsilon}\right).

Then, by
Eq.

10.25
, for small enough
\kappa
,

\mathbb{E}_{g}^{\mathrm{\scriptsize{WP}}}\big[\hat{H}_{L,m}(r_{1})\,\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\kappa}}\big]=\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon,\kappa}\left(g^{1+5\epsilon}+g^{1+3\epsilon}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\epsilon,\kappa}\left(g^{1+5\epsilon}\right)

which is exactly what was needed to conclude in light of

Eq.

10.20
.
∎
